# Counterfeit and spurious medicine vendors in India, Jan 2025 to 7 October 2026: cases, parties, persons and FIRs (short list)

Compiled 7 October 2026 from police, regulator and press reports. Every row names the party (firm) and the person(s) reported with it. Names of accused persons are allegations from the cited reports unless a court has convicted. This short list holds only the hospital-supply and major-racket cases (60 of 166); the full report file has every case, the CDSCO batch tables and all sources.

## Bottom line

The full file holds 166 cases. 25 of them involve hospitals, clinics or government hospital supply chains directly. The four that matter most for a hospital pharmacy chain are below.

- **Bengaluru / Bidadi ICU-drug racket (Aug to Oct 2026).** Party: Krupa Healthcare, near Minerva Circle, Bengaluru, and an unlicensed repacking farmhouse at Hejjala/Dodderi near Bidadi toll gate. Persons: Veeresh Kumar Jain (owner of Krupa Healthcare, arrested 12 Sep 2026) and Prashanth Ranjan Babu, also reported as R. Prashanth (alleged kingpin, ex-medical representative, arrested at Kempegowda airport). Counterfeit ICU injections (Cefiavigent 2.5, Hizlam, Emblaveo with a Pfizer label) and cancer drugs were sold at discounts of up to 50 percent to more than 90 hospitals, clinics and shops through 15 or more medical representatives. Karnataka cancelled 16 distribution licences and suspended 8, including a Pfizer C&F depot. Suspect batch numbers are blocked on the Karnataka FDA portal and circulated to all states.
- **Delhi Crime Branch anti-cancer vial syndicate (FIR 12 Jul 2026, 17 arrested 20 Aug 2026).** Persons: Abhishek Vaishya, Shubham Kumar Chaudhary, Jaswant Sharma (oncology nursing in-charge of a hospital, who removed vials from the ward) and 14 others. Products: refilled or diverted Keytruda, Darzalex, Imfinzi, Erbitux, Opdyta, Gazyva, Bavencio and Adcetris vials, plus government supply diverted from PBM Hospital, Bikaner. No batch numbers were published.
- **Fake Alburel human albumin 20 percent, Maharashtra (three FIRs, Sep 2025 to Apr 2026).** Parties and persons: Shriram Healthcare, Pune (Shriram Nandkishor Chandak); R.S.L. Pharma, Pune (Rajesh Shantaram Jog); R.R.T. Pharma, Panvel; Maitri Pharmaceuticals and four distributors (Pune Vishrambaug FIR); Hitashi Pharmaceuticals and distributors (Nagpur Shantinagar FIR). Fake batches AM20025077 and AN20G25084 were seized. RituxiRel was also sampled.
- **Jackson Laboratories, Amritsar, TOCIN oxytocin batch I-7881 with nil oxytocin (May to Jun 2026).** Person: Managing Director Jugal Kishore. Five post-caesarean maternal deaths at Kota government hospitals; distributor Rajasthan Medical Hall, Kota, lost its licence; Punjab and Himachal manufacturing licences cancelled and a nationwide recall issued. Jackson batches also appeared in the Sep 2026 RML Hospital, Delhi, recall.

Also on the hospital radar: the Coldrif cough syrup diethylene glycol deaths (Sresan Pharmaceutical, Kancheepuram; owner G. Ranganathan; batch SR-13), the Lucknow cross-billing FIR against Health Plus (Ankur Awasthi), MK Pharma (Manish Bajpai), Ram Pharma (Govind Shukla) and Patient Care Surgical House, Saharanpur (Aijaz Ahmed), and the Puducherry fake Lupin and Sun Pharma brand racket run by N. Raja alias Valliappan, now with the CBI.

**Most urgent action:** pull every ICU injectable, oncology vial, human albumin, oxytocin and paediatric cough syrup bought since January 2025 from any party in Section 1 or Section 2, or from any non-authorised stockist at an unusual discount, into quarantine today, and verify batch numbers with the manufacturer before further use. Section 6 has the full checklist.

## 3 to 5. Case files, regulator alerts and cough-syrup cases

See the full report file (fake-medicine-vendors-india-2025-2026.md) for the state-by-state case files, the CDSCO spurious and NSQ batch tables, licence cancellations and blacklists, brand-owner notices and the cough-syrup cases.

## 6. What to do now in your hospital pharmacies

**Quarantine and batch-check (today)**

1. Print Section 1 and Section 2. Check every purchase invoice since 1 January 2025 against the party names and the person names (owners and medical representatives). Any match: move the stock to a sealed quarantine shelf and stop dispensing.
2. Pull and hold, regardless of vendor: all human albumin 20 percent (Alburel batches AM20025077 and AN20G25084 were fake), all oxytocin (TOCIN batch I-7881 and 1-8386 from Jackson Laboratories), Coldrif batch SR-13, Respifresh TR batch R01GL2523, ReLife batch LSL25160, any Cefiavigent, Hizlam or Emblaveo injection not bought from the authorised Pfizer or manufacturer stockist, and any oncology vial (Keytruda, Opdivo, Opdyta, Darzalex, Imfinzi, Erbitux, Gazyva, Bavencio, Adcetris, Herceptin, Avastin) bought outside the manufacturer-authorised channel.
3. Compare the batch numbers of everything on the shelves against the batch column in Sections 1 and 4 and against the CDSCO monthly alerts (cdsco.gov.in, Notifications, Alerts). Karnataka has blocked the suspect Bidadi batches on its FDA portal and circulated them to all states; ask your state drug controller for that list.

**Verify before releasing stock**

1. Call the manufacturer's customer-care or anti-counterfeit helpline with the batch number, manufacturing date and expiry date. Pfizer, Roche, MSD, Glenmark, Alkem, Torrent, Sun Pharma, GSK and Reliance Life Sciences all have brand verification channels; Section 4 lists the notices found.
2. Scan the QR or barcode on the pack. Under the Schedule H2 rule the top 300 brands must carry a unique code with manufacturer, batch, expiry and licence details. A code that does not scan, scans to a different product, or repeats across packs is a red flag (Patna IGIMS case: two un-scannable branded medicines were held).
3. Check the supplier's drug licence number on every invoice against the state drug controller's licence register, and the GST number on the GST portal. The Lucknow and Delhi rackets ran on fake GST firms and cross-billing. A licence that is cancelled or suspended (Karnataka: 16 cancelled, 8 suspended) means the stock cannot be legitimately traced.
4. Physical checks for injectables and vials: crimp seal and flip-off cap integrity, printing quality and font, label alignment, expiry format, vial fill level and clarity, carton glue and perforations. Refilled vials were the method in the Delhi oncology syndicate; relabelled expired stock in new containers was the method at Bidadi.

**If you find suspect stock**

1. Do not return it to the vendor and do not accept a replacement or credit note. Seal it, label it "suspect, do not dispense", and keep it in the pharmacy under lock.
2. Inform the state drug inspector in writing the same day and ask them to draw samples under Section 23 of the Drugs and Cosmetics Act. Report on the CDSCO portal (cdsco.gov.in, Report, spurious or adverse drug reports) and on the state Drugs Control Administration helpline.
3. Keep the purchase order, invoice, delivery challan, payment proof, the vendor's licence copy and the names of the medical representatives who sold it. Investigators in the Karnataka case served notices on more than 25 hospitals and asked exactly for this.
4. Tell the treating doctors which patients received which batch, and watch for therapeutic failure (infection not responding, albumin not raising levels, oxytocin not producing contraction) and unusual reactions. Record these as adverse drug reactions with the hospital pharmacovigilance committee.

**Purchase-side rules going forward**

1. Buy only from the manufacturer's authorised C&F agent or stockist. Ask each vendor for the manufacturer's authorisation letter and the chain of custody for high-value injectables.
2. Treat any offer of 30 to 60 percent below the stockist price for cancer drugs, ICU antibiotics, albumin, IVIG, insulin or vaccines as a counterfeit signal. Every racket in this file sold at a deep discount.
3. Insist on batch-wise invoices with manufacturer name, batch, manufacturing and expiry dates, and reconcile them with the manufacturer's own supply record when the value is high.
4. Rotate a monthly check of your vendor list against the CDSCO alerts, your state drug controller's cancellation orders, and the state medical services corporation blacklist (Karnataka KSMSCL, Telangana TGMSIDC, Uttar Pradesh UPMSCL, Madhya Pradesh MPPHSCL and Kerala KMSCL all blacklisted suppliers in this period).
5. Keep physician samples out of saleable stock and keep government-supply packs ("Not for sale") out entirely; both were resold by rackets in Agra, Delhi and Patna.

## 7. Coverage and caveats

- **Cases:** 166 after merging duplicates. Priority split: 25 hospital-supply, 35 major rackets, 66 regulatory alerts and licence actions, 40 other.
- **Evidence levels:** confirmed 83 (core facts seen on two or more opened pages), partially-confirmed 49 (one opened page, or some details unsupported), unverifiable 34 (no page could be opened; facts come from search-result summaries only). Treat unverifiable rows as leads to check, not findings.
- **Period:** January 2025 to 7 October 2026, with a few older cases kept because they shaped 2025-2026 enforcement (Ballari ringer lactate deaths, Nov 2024; Mira Road civic hospital, 2022).
- **CDSCO alerts:** the monthly alert PDFs were read directly only for January and February 2025 (10 spurious entries, 131 hospital-relevant NSQ entries). The remaining months to August 2026 are covered through press summaries of each alert (see the CDSCO case ids in Section 3). Batch-level NSQ lists for those months should be pulled from cdsco.gov.in before any stock audit.
- **What was cut short:** a direct read of state drug-control websites, the Karnataka Drugs Control public notices and a second deep-dive on the Bidadi case were interrupted by an account usage limit on 7 October 2026. The Bidadi case therefore rests on press reports and is marked partially-confirmed.
- **Search limits:** early search rounds ran under a per-turn web-search budget and a network block on news and government sites; the block was lifted later and every case was then re-checked by opening its sources.
- **Legal status:** this is news- and regulator-reported information, not legal advice. Named persons are accused, not convicted, unless the row says so.

Full source list: Section 8 of the full report file.
